# Day 1 — Full Audio Transcript (machine-transcribed)

**Source:** complete 8h45m broadcast recording, transcribed locally with faster-whisper (small model, int8).
**Timestamps:** [hh:mm:ss] from the start of the broadcast. Wall-clock Pacific ≈ timestamp + 8:30 AM (broadcast opened ~8:30 AM PDT).
**Accuracy note:** machine transcription — expect word errors, especially product names ("Grok Bot", "Cursor", "P-Stack") and proper nouns. Speaker labels are not identified by the model; identify speakers from context (opening intros, session handoffs, guest segments).
**Garble decoder:** "Graphot/Grockbot/Brock bot/GROC bot/GrogFod" = Grok Bot; "SpaceX AI" = xAI; "Versailles" = Vercel; "P-Stack" ≈ their stack tooling; "Contra in capital" ≈ Contrarian Capital.

## Session map (audio time → content)

- 00:00–00:25 — Opening: intros (Lauren, Roshan, Matt Palmer), run of show, contest announcement
- 00:25–1:44 — Grok Bot 101 (Roman and Rita): what Grok Bot is, onboarding, marketplace, email demo
- 1:44–2:30 — Idea selection → **restaurant pop-up** chosen ("Pop-up OS"); guest Cody Sanchez joins (~2:55): distribution, controversy marketing, proof vault; live creation of a marketing bot
- 2:30–4:30 — Grok Bot for Engineering (Lingxi Li): integrations (GitHub/Notion/Figma/Slack), Cursor Cloud Agents, multi-agent PR→deploy→review; meanwhile the trio buys a domain, builds the lander, sets "ship to main" rules
- 4:30–5:30 — Grok Bot for Product Managers (Kevin Niparko): agent teams, scoped memory, parallelism
- 5:30–8:45 — Grok Bot for Founders (Shub Gaur): AI Maturity Curve, Why Grok Bot, Founder Use Cases, sales call prep, Optimizing Costs; pop-up build continues (night-market ticket stubs, badges/lanyards, plush designs, SEO work order); close at 1.1M views

[05:57:13] is grounded in this idea that we should really have these agentic colleagues that we can work with on all the great things we do when we build products. And so that was really the motivation. So really, I think there's four things that we kind of took as the brief based on that colleague definition. The first one is that colleagues, when you work with them, often tie multiple tools together.
[05:57:35] They don't live in one system. They work across many systems. And those systems do different things, right? So you might have a ticketing system, like I mentioned, linear or JIRA or something like it. You might be using CRM or CRM++, something like Salesforce or more. You might be keeping documents in Notion, designs in Figma. And all these tools are really, really great at what they do. But oftentimes, your colleagues or yourselves are tying things together across them.
